<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that approximating the electronic Coulomb spectral infimum in three-dimensional space is QMA-hard when positive integer nuclear charges are encoded in binary. The nuclei have distinct rational positions, the electron number is unary, and the energy is minimized over all antisymmetric continuum states and spin sectors. A deterministic classical polynomial-time reduction produces instances with threshold separation at least one. The nuclear charges may be exponentially large, but every output has polynomial bit length.

- **claim-main-qma-hardness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The promise problem above is QMA-hard under deterministic classical polynomial-time many-one reductions for $c=1$. The reduction can produce instances with $b-a\ge1$.
  - Explanation: The exact clamped-nuclei electronic promise problem with binary-encoded positive integer charges is QMA-hard under deterministic classical polynomial-time many-one reductions, and the reduction can output instances whose threshold separation is at least one.
- **claim-source-heisenberg** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-source)): There is a QMA-complete promise problem whose Hamiltonians are $$H_{\rm in}=\sum_{e=LR}J_e h^s_{LR}$$ on finite, nonwrapping subgraphs of the square lattice, with no one-qubit terms. The signed weights $J_e$ are rational. The number of vertices and edges, the lattice extent, the weight magnitudes, and the threshold magnitudes are bounded by fixed polynomials in the source size $n\geq2$. The rational thresholds $a_s<b_s$ have separation $\delta_s=b_s-a_s\geq1/\operatorname{poly}(n)$.
  - Explanation: The imported hardness input is a QMA-complete promise problem for signed isotropic Heisenberg Hamiltonians on finite square-lattice subgraphs without one-qubit terms, with polynomially bounded sizes and inverse-polynomial threshold separation.
- **claim-second-order-bottom-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-second-order)): Let $A\geq0$ be a finite-dimensional self-adjoint operator, let $P\neq0$ project onto its kernel, and put $Q=I-P$. Suppose $A\geq gQ$ for some $g>0$. Let $B=B^*$ satisfy $PBP=0$ and $\lVert B\rVert\leq\epsilon g$, where $0\leq\epsilon<1/2$. Define $A^{-1}$ to vanish on $P$. Then $$\label{eq:second-order-bound} \left\|E(A+B)-E\bigl(-PBA^{-1}BP\big\|_{P}\bigr)\right\| \leq 2g\epsilon^3.$$ The conclusion also holds when $Q=0$.
  - Explanation: For a gapped nonnegative operator with a block-off-diagonal perturbation, the bottom energy equals the second-order effective bottom energy up to an error controlled cubically by the relative perturbation size.
- **claim-positive-spin-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-spin)): If $\sqrt\Delta>3V$, then $$\label{eq:positive-spin-error} \bigl\|E(H_+)-E(H_{\rm in})+C_0\bigr\| \leq\frac{27V^3}{\sqrt\Delta}.$$ In particular, a polynomially large integer $\Delta$ makes this error an arbitrarily small prescribed inverse polynomial.
  - Explanation: Singlet-mediator ancilla pairs turn the signed Heisenberg Hamiltonian into one with only positive couplings plus an explicit scalar shift, with an error that shrinks as the ancilla coupling scale grows.
- **claim-contact-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-contact-layout)): All the sites $p_i$ are distinct. Every pair is either at distance one or at distance at least $\sqrt2$. The complete set $\mathcal C$ of unit-distance pairs consists of:* 1. *$L A_e$, $A_e R$, and $A_e B_e$ for a negative source edge;* 2. *$L A_e$, $A_e B_e$, and $B_e R$ for a positive source edge;* 3. *$B_e A_f$ for two consecutive positive source edges $e,f$ on the same original lattice line.* *Thus every interaction of $H_+$ is a contact. Its weight on a contact will be denoted $K_{ij}\geq0$, using $K_{ij}=0$ for contacts in the third class.
  - Explanation: All sites are distinct and every pair is either a unit-distance contact or at least a fixed larger distance apart; the contacts are listed completely, so every interaction of the positive Hamiltonian is a contact.
- **claim-contact-displacement** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-displacement)): For every real assignment $(d_{ij})_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal C}$ there are displacements $s_i\in\mathbb R^3$ such that $$\label{eq:contact-displacement} u_{ij}\cdot(s_j-s_i)=d_{ij},\qquad \max_i\|s_i\|\leq6m\max_{\mathcal C}\|d_{ij}\|.$$ For an empty contact set take all displacements zero. The construction is linear in $d$, uses polynomially many arithmetic operations with coefficients in $\mathbb Q(\sqrt3)$, and has no consistency condition around lattice cycles.
  - Explanation: A linear right inverse converts arbitrary prescribed first-order contact length changes into bounded site displacements, with no consistency condition around lattice cycles.
- **claim-hubbard-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-hubbard)): The construction above is deterministic and polynomial time. With $$\label{eq:finite-scalar-shift} C_s=C_0+\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal C}K_{ij} =3k\Delta+\frac32\sum_e v_e^2 +\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal C}K_{ij},$$ it satisfies $$\label{eq:finite-combined-error} \left\|E\bigl(H_{\rm Hub}(t)\bigr) -\alpha\bigl(E(H_{\rm in})-C_s\bigr)\right\| \leq\frac{3\alpha\gamma}{64}.$$ The scalar $C_s$ has polynomial magnitude and can be approximated to any polynomial-bit absolute precision in polynomial time.
  - Explanation: A half-filled Hubbard Hamiltonian with rational positive hoppings and fixed onsite repulsion reproduces the positive Heisenberg energy, up to an explicit scalar shift and scale, with error a small fraction of the promise gap.
- **claim-isolated-well-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-isolated-well)): The operators $h_i$ are self-adjoint on $H^2(\mathbb R^3)$, with form domain $H^1(\mathbb R^3)$. Each has a simple ground eigenvalue $\lambda_i$ and a real, positive, normalized eigenfunction $\psi_i$. There is a constant $g>0$ such that the remaining spectrum is at least $\lambda_i+g$. For $\phi_i(y)=\pi^{-1/2}e^{-r_i}$, $$\|\lambda_i+1/2\|\le C\frac mD,\qquad \\|\psi_i-\phi_i\\|_{H^2}\le C\frac mD. \label{eq:wells-h2}$$ Moreover, for derivative orders $j=0,1,2$, $$\\|D^jW_i\\|_\infty\le C\frac m{D^{j+1}}, \qquad 0\le W_i\le\frac{100m}{D}. \label{eq:wells-field-bounds}$$ These assertions hold before tuning and uniformly throughout $[1,4]^m$.
  - Explanation: Each capped single-well operator is self-adjoint with a simple ground level, a uniform gap, a ground state close to the hydrogenic orbital, and capped remote fields with uniform derivative bounds.
- **claim-wells-secants** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-wells-secants)): With centers and rounded charges fixed, for every choice of the other parameters, $\lambda_i(\rho_i=1)<-1/2<\lambda_i(\rho_i=4)$. For $1\le a<b\le4$, holding the other parameters fixed, $$c q'(b-a)\le\lambda_i(b)-\lambda_i(a)\le Cq'(b-a). \label{eq:wells-own-slope}$$ If instead $\rho_i$ is held fixed, then $$\|\lambda_i(\rho)-\lambda_i(\widetilde\rho)\| \le Cq'\frac m{D^2}\\|\rho-\widetilde\rho\\|_\infty. \label{eq:wells-other-slope}$$
  - Explanation: Moving the secondary nucleus brackets the target energy, and the ground energy varies with uniform two-sided secant bounds in the own parameter and weakly in the other parameters.
- **claim-well-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-well-tail)): Uniformly for all $\rho\in[1,4]^m$, $$\|\psi_i(y)\|\le C\exp[-.99(r_i-.01D)], \label{eq:wells-tail}$$ and, for sufficiently large $D$, $$\\|\psi_i\\|_{H^1(\{r_i>.65D\})}\le e^{-.61D}. \label{eq:wells-h1-tail}$$
  - Explanation: Before tuning, single-well ground states have uniform exponential decay away from the core and an exponentially small exterior H^1 norm.
- **claim-wells-analytic** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-wells-analytic)): Suppose $u$ is bounded on the unit ball in $\mathbb R^3$ and satisfies $\Delta u=Fu$ there, where $$\\|\partial^\beta F\\|_\infty\le A^{\|\beta\|+1}\|\beta\|! \quad\text{for every multi-index }\beta. \label{eq:wells-analytic-coefficient}$$ There is a constant $A_1$, depending only on $A$, such that $$\max_{\|\alpha\|=k}\\|\partial^\alpha u\\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/4})} \le \\|u\\|_{L^2(B_1)}A_1^{k+1}k!. \label{eq:wells-analytic-derivatives}$$ On a sufficiently small fixed ball about the origin, Taylor polynomials consequently have geometrically decreasing errors in both value and first derivative, with constants depending only on $A$.
  - Explanation: Solutions of Laplace-type equations with analytic coefficients have factorially controlled derivatives, so Taylor polynomials on small balls converge geometrically.
- **claim-ritz-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-wells-ritz-approximation)): For each $i$, each rational choice of centers and secondary parameters, and each sufficiently large $D$, one can explicitly construct a space $\mathcal V_i\subset H^1(\mathbb R^3)$ of rational piecewise polynomials, of dimension polynomial in $D$, supported in $r_i<.8D$, such that $$\inf_{v\in\mathcal V_i}\\|v-\psi_i\\|_{H^1}\le e^{-.58D}. \label{eq:wells-ritz-h1}$$ Its lowest Ritz value $\lambda_i^{\mathrm R}$ satisfies $$0\le\lambda_i^{\mathrm R}-\lambda_i\le C e^{-1.16D}. \label{eq:wells-ritz-bias}$$ The construction and constants are uniform over rational parameters throughout $[1,4]^m$.
  - Explanation: An explicit polynomial-dimension graded space of rational piecewise polynomials approximates the single-well ground state in H^1 with exponential accuracy, and its lowest Ritz value is a one-sided exponentially accurate approximation of the eigenvalue.
- **claim-ritz-oracle** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-ritz-oracle)): Suppose the centers and the parameters in $[1,4]^m$ are rational, and all their numerators and denominators, as well as those of $q,q'$, have at most $B$ bits. Under the hypotheses of this section, a deterministic algorithm returns rational numbers $\ell_i\le\lambda_i\le u_i$ with $$u_i-\ell_i\le\varepsilon_D,\qquad \varepsilon_D=2^{-\lceil8D/5\rceil}<e^{-1.1D}. \label{eq:wells-oracle-precision}$$ Its bit complexity is polynomial in $D,m,B$. The bound is uniform over every rational sample in $[1,4]^m$; no spectral gap of a Ritz matrix is assumed.
  - Explanation: A deterministic procedure returns certified rational lower and upper enclosures of a single-well eigenvalue to exponential precision in polynomial bit complexity, without assuming any gap of a Ritz matrix.
- **claim-simultaneous-tuning** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-tuning)): At any fixed rational centers satisfying the hypotheses of this section, and with the integer charges in <a href="#eq:wells-charges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:wells-charges">[eq:wells-charges]</a> already fixed, one can compute rational $\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_m\in[1,4]$ such that $$\|\lambda_i+1/2\|\le e^{-1.01D}\qquad(1\le i\le m). \label{eq:wells-tuned}$$ The algorithm is deterministic and polynomial in $D,m$ and the input bit lengths. Every parameter it samples or outputs has $O(D)$ fractional bits.
  - Explanation: Certified bisection inside a contracting simultaneous iteration computes rational secondary offsets that tune every well energy to the target within an exponentially small error in polynomial time.
- **claim-relative-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-relative-tail)): Suppose the actual rounded charges and the parameters $\rho_i$ obey the conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:tuning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tuning">14</a>, in particular $\|\lambda_i+1/2\|\le e^{-1.01D}$. Uniformly for $r_i\le2D$, $$\psi_i(y)=\pi^{-1/2}e^{-r_i+F_i(y)} \bigl(1+O(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\bigr). \label{eq:relative-tail}$$
  - Explanation: For tuned wells the ground state equals the hydrogenic exponential corrected by a ray primitive of the remote field, with small relative error, which is what overlap estimates at exponentially small scale need.
- **claim-surrogate-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-surrogate)): The zero-extended kernels $$G_D(z)=\frac{\chi(\|z\|/D)}{\|z\|},\qquad J_{D,\rho}(z)=\frac{\chi(\|z\|/D)}{\|z-\rho e_z\|}$$ are globally $C^2$, uniformly for $1\le\rho\le4$. For every multi-index $\alpha$ with $\|\alpha\|\le2$, $$\\|\partial^\alpha G_D\\|_\infty+ \\|\partial^\alpha J_{D,\rho}\\|_\infty \le C D^{-1-\|\alpha\|}. \label{eq:capped-kernel-bounds}$$ Uniformly over all allowed secondary positions and rounded charges, $$\\|W_i-W_i^*\\|_\infty\le\frac{Cm^2}{D^2},\qquad \|F_i(y)-F_i^*(y)\|\le\frac{Cm^2}{D}\quad(r_i\le3D). \label{eq:surrogate-error}$$ Consequently, for tuned wells the relative formula <a href="#eq:relative-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:relative-tail">[eq:relative-tail]</a> holds with $F_i^*$ in place of $F_i$ and the same error bound $O(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})$.
  - Explanation: The capped kernels are globally smooth with uniform derivative bounds, and replacing the true remote field by an explicit surrogate depending only on primary centers costs only a small relative error uniformly over the tuning cube.
- **claim-positive-hopping-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-hopping)): For tuned wells and every contact, $$B_{ij}=T_{ij}(X) \bigl(1+O(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\bigr). \label{eq:hole-surrogate-comparison}$$ The constants are uniform in $\rho_j\in[1,4]$.
  - Explanation: The oriented hole attraction integral agrees with an explicit positive hopping integral up to a small relative error.
- **claim-reference-hopping-size** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reference-hopping)): Let $X^0=(Dp_i)_i$. For every contact, $$c e^{-D}\le T_{ij}(X^0)\le C D^2 e^{-D+Cm}. \label{eq:reference-hopping-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: At the undisplaced reference layout every contact integral is bounded above and below by explicit exponentially small quantities.
- **claim-hopping-displacement-law** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hopping-displacement)): Let $X_i=Dp_i+s_i$ with $\|s_i\|\le S$. There is a constant $c_1>0$ such that, uniformly on all contacts, $$\begin{aligned} \frac{T_{ij}(X)}{T_{ij}(X^0)} &=e^{-u_{ij}\cdot(s_j-s_i)} \bigl(1+O(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disp}})\bigr), \label{eq:hopping-displacement-law}\\ \varepsilon_{\mathrm{disp}} &=\frac{mS}{D}+\frac{S^2}{D} +S D^{-1/4}+e^{-c_1\sqrt D}. \label{eq:hopping-displacement-error} \end{aligned}$$ In particular the coefficient of the projected displacement in the leading exponent is one.
  - Explanation: Displacing the centers multiplies a contact integral by the exponential of minus the projected displacement difference, with coefficient exactly one in the exponent and small relative error.
- **claim-hopping-quadrature** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-hopping-quadrature)): For $0<\epsilon<1/10$, one can compute a positive rational $\widehat I_{ij}$ satisfying $$\left\|\log\frac{\widehat I_{ij}}{e^D T_{ij}(X^0)}\right\| \le\epsilon \label{eq:reference-quadrature-accuracy}$$ by a deterministic algorithm polynomial in $D$, $m$, the bit lengths of the layout data, and $\epsilon^{-1}$. In particular the algorithm is polynomial in the source size when $D$ is a fixed polynomial and $\epsilon$ an inverse polynomial.
  - Explanation: The positive reference integrals can be computed to relative accuracy by a deterministic polynomial-time quadrature that controls the logarithm of the exponential factor and integrates the nonnegative weight exactly.
- **claim-hopping-orientation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hopping-orientation)): For distinct sites, $$B_{ij}-B_{ji} =(\lambda_i-\lambda_j)\langle\psi_j,\psi_i\rangle. \label{eq:hopping-orientation-identity}$$ For tuned wells and large $D$ under the stated separation bounds, $$\|\langle\psi_j,\psi_i\rangle\|\le e^{-0.85D},\qquad \|B_{ij}-B_{ji}\|\le2e^{-1.86D}. \label{eq:hopping-orientation-error}$$
  - Explanation: The difference of opposite oriented attraction integrals is exactly the eigenvalue difference times the orbital overlap, and for tuned wells it is exponentially small.
- **claim-hopping-calibration** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-calibration)): Let the contact layout be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:contact-layout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:contact-layout">5</a>, choose one orientation per contact, and let the prescribed rational hoppings satisfy $0<t_{\min}\le t_{ij}\le1$, where $t_{\min}$ is an inverse polynomial in the source size. Let $Z_*$ be an integer with $e^D/2\le Z_*\le2e^D$ and round the nuclear charges as in Section <a href="#sec:wells" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wells">3</a>. For inverse-polynomial $\tau$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time procedure that computes rational centers $X_i=Dp_i+s_i$ obeying $$S:=\max_i\|s_i\| \le Cm\bigl(m+\log D+\log(1/t_{\min})+1\bigr)+\tau. \label{eq:calibrated-displacement-size}$$ After applying Proposition <a href="#prop:tuning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tuning">14</a> at these final centers, the resulting orbitals satisfy, on every chosen oriented contact, $$\|Z_*B_{ij}-t_{ij}\| \le C t_{ij}\mathcal E_{\mathrm{cal}}. \label{eq:calibrated-contact-error}$$ Here $D$ is taken sufficiently large for the hypotheses above and for $\mathcal E_{\mathrm{cal}}$ to be small. The reverse oriented attraction has an additional error at most $4e^{-0.86D}$ in the same rescaled units. No additional geometric equation for the reverse orientation is needed.
  - Explanation: A deterministic polynomial-time procedure places rational centers so that, after tuning, the rescaled attraction integrals match the prescribed hoppings with small relative error, without any nonlinear solve.
- **claim-one-body-matrix** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-body-matrix)): For the preceding parameters and sufficiently large $D$, $$\\|A\psi_i\\|_2\le e^{-.75D},\qquad \\|G-I\\|\le e^{-.85D}. \label{eq:continuum-residual-gram}$$ In particular $G$ is positive definite. The isometry $\mathcal J_0=\mathcal U G^{-1/2}$ has orthonormal columns $\omega_i=\mathcal J_0e_i$, spanning the same spatial orbital space. Its matrix satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \\|Z_*\mathcal J_0^*A\mathcal J_0-\mathsf T\\| &\le \delta_1, \label{eq:continuum-one-body-error}\\ \delta_1 &:=m\epsilon_{\mathrm{hop}}+Z_*e^{-1.01D} +CmZ_*e^{-1.1D}+CmZ_*e^{-1.6D}. \notag \end{aligned}$$ If $P_0=\mathcal J_0\mathcal J_0^*$, then $$\\|AP_0\\|\le C\sqrt m\,e^{-.75D}, \qquad \\|\omega_i-\phi_i\\|_{H^1}\le Cm/D. \label{eq:continuum-projected-residual}$$
  - Explanation: The site orbitals have exponentially small residual and Gram deviation, so the orthonormalized one-particle matrix is close to the target hopping matrix.
- **claim-complement-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-complement-gap)): There is a constant $g>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $D$, $$(I-P)A(I-P)\ge g(I-P).$$ The inequality holds on the full one-particle form domain, including functions supported between the nuclei or at infinity.
  - Explanation: The one-particle operator has a uniform positive gap on the whole orthogonal complement of the site orbitals, including functions supported between the nuclei or at infinity.
- **claim-fermion-hardy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fermion-hardy)): Uniformly over all normalized $u\in\operatorname{Ran}\Pi$, $$\sum_{\ell=1}^m\\|\nabla_{y_\ell}u\\|_2^2\le Cm, \qquad \\|W_{\mathrm{ee}}\Pi\\|\le Cm^2. \label{eq:continuum-fermion-hardy}$$ The constants do not depend on the dimension $\binom{2m}{m}$ of $\operatorname{Ran}\Pi$.
  - Explanation: On the many-fermion subspace built from the site orbitals, the kinetic energy and the electron repulsion obey bounds independent of the dimension of that subspace.
- **claim-continuum-elimination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-continuum-elimination)): For sufficiently large $D$, let $E_\Pi=\min\operatorname{Spec} (\Pi\mathcal H\Pi\|_{\operatorname{Ran}\Pi})$. Then $$0\le E_\Pi-\inf\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal H \le e^{-1.2D}. \label{eq:continuum-leakage-error}$$ More explicitly, the upper bound can be taken to be $$C\left(m^{3/2}e^{-.75D}+m^2/Z_*\right)^2.$$
  - Explanation: Completing a square against the complement gap shows that the full spectral infimum differs from the compressed minimum by an exponentially small amount, a genuine lower bound for all states.
- **claim-coulomb-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-continuum-coulomb-compression)): For sufficiently large $D$, $$\left\\|W_{\mathrm{eff}} -U\sum_{i=1}^m n_{i\uparrow}n_{i\downarrow}\right\\| \le \frac{Cm^5}{D},\qquad U=\frac58 . \label{eq:continuum-coulomb-error}$$
  - Explanation: In the orthonormal site modes the electron repulsion reduces to the onsite Hubbard term with a polynomially small error in the inverse scale.
- **claim-continuum-simulation** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-continuum-simulation)): Let $E_0=\inf\operatorname{Spec}H$ for the physical molecular Hamiltonian and let $E_{\mathrm{Hub}}(t)$ be the minimum of the half-filled Hubbard Hamiltonian in Proposition <a href="#prop:hubbard" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:hubbard">7</a>. The construction satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \left\|\frac{E_0}{Z_*}+\frac{mZ_*}{2} -E_{\mathrm{Hub}}(t)\right\| &\le m\delta_1+\frac{Cm^5}{D}+Z_*e^{-1.2D} \label{eq:continuum-simulation-units}\\ &\le C\biggl[ m^2\epsilon_{\mathrm{hop}}+\frac{m^5}{D} +m e^{-.01D}+m^2e^{-.1D}\notag\\ &\hspace{35mm}{}+m^2e^{-.6D}+e^{-.2D} \biggr].\label{eq:continuum-simulation-final} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, for every prescribed fixed inverse-polynomial error budget $\epsilon_*$, the polynomial scale and calibration precision can be chosen so that the left side is at most $\epsilon_*$.
  - Explanation: The physical infimum, rescaled and shifted, matches the Hubbard minimum within an error that can be made any fixed inverse polynomial by the choice of scale and calibration precision.

```json
          "exact_quote": "For every real assignment $(d_{ij})_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\mathcal C}$ there are displacements $s_i\\in\\mathbb R^3$ such that $$\\label{eq:contact-displacement}\n u_{ij}\\cdot(s_j-s_i)=d_{ij},\\qquad\n \\max_i|s_i|\\leq6m\\max_{\\mathcal C}|d_{ij}|.$$ For an empty contact set take all displacements zero.         }

        "exact_quote": "The operators $h_i$ are self-adjoint on $H^2(\\mathbb R^3)$, with form domain $H^1(\\mathbb R^3)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-wells-analytic",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $u$ is bounded on the unit ball in $\\mathbb R^3$ and satisfies $\\Delta u=Fu$ there, where $$\\|\\partial^\\beta F\\|_\\infty\\le A^{|\\beta|+1}|\\beta|!\n \\quad\\text{for every multi-index }\\beta.\n \\label{eq:wells-analytic-coefficient}$$ There is a constant $A_1$, depending only on $A$, such that $$\\max_{|\\alpha|=k}\\|\\partial^\\alpha u\\|_{L^\\infty(B_{1/4})}\n \\le \\|u\\|_{L^2(B_1)}A_1^{k+1}k!.\n \\label{eq:wells-analytic-derivatives}$$ On a sufficiently small fixed ball about the origin, Taylor polynomials consequently have geometrically decreasing errors in both value and first derivative, with constants depending only on $A$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-wells-analytic",

        "exact_quote": "The zero-extended kernels $$G_D(z)=\\frac{\\chi(|z|/D)}{|z|},\\qquad\n J_{D,\\rho}(z)=\\frac{\\chi(|z|/D)}{|z-\\rho e_z|}$$ are globally $C^2$, uniformly for $1\\le\\rho\\le4$.         "exact_quote": "Let the contact layout be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:contact-layout\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:contact-layout\">5</a>, choose one orientation per contact, and let the prescribed rational hoppings satisfy $0<t_{\\min}\\le t_{ij}\\le1$, where $t_{\\min}$ is an inverse polynomial in the source size. Let $Z_*$ be an integer with $e^D/2\\le Z_*\\le2e^D$ and round the nuclear charges as in Section <a href=\"#sec:wells\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:wells\">3</a>. For inverse-polynomial $\\tau$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time procedure that computes rational centers $X_i=Dp_i+s_i$ obeying $$S:=\\max_i|s_i|\n   \\le Cm\\bigl(m+\\log D+\\log(1/t_{\\min})+1\\bigr)+\\tau.\n \\label{eq:calibrated-displacement-size}$$ After applying Proposition <a href=\"#prop:tuning\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tuning\">14</a> at these final centers, the resulting orbitals satisfy, on every chosen oriented contact, $$|Z_*B_{ij}-t_{ij}|\n      \\le C t_{ij}\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{cal}}.\n \\label{eq:calibrated-contact-error}$$ Here $D$ is taken sufficiently large for the hypotheses above and for $\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{cal}}$ to be small.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-one-body-matrix",
        "exact_quote": "For the preceding parameters and sufficiently large $D$, $$\\|A\\psi_i\\|_2\\le e^{-.75D},\\qquad\n \\|G-I\\|\\le e^{-.85D}.\n \\label{eq:continuum-residual-gram}$$ In particular $G$ is positive definite. The isometry $\\mathcal J_0=\\mathcal U G^{-1/2}$ has orthonormal columns $\\omega_i=\\mathcal J_0e_i$, spanning the same spatial orbital space. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **spectral infimum E_0** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Set $$E_0(R,Z,N)=\inf\operatorname{Spec}H(R,Z,N).$$
- **total input length L** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $L$ for the total bit length and require $b-a\ge L^{-c}$.
- **Heisenberg exchange operator** ([source](#sec:finite)): On two spin-$1/2$ sites set $$h^s_{ij}=X_iX_j+Y_iY_j+Z_iZ_j =\boldsymbol\sigma_i\cdot\boldsymbol\sigma_j.$$

```json
          },
```

```json
            "anchor": "eq:hubbard-definition",
            "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:hubbard-definition}\n \\begin{split}\n H_{\\rm Hub}(t)\n   &=-\\sum_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\mathcal C}\\sum_{\\sigma=\\uparrow,\\downarrow}\n       t_{ij}\\bigl(c_{i\\sigma}^\\dagger c_{j\\sigma}\n                  +c_{j\\sigma}^\\dagger c_{i\\sigma}\\bigr)\n       +U\\sum_i n_{i\\uparrow}n_{i\\downarrow},\\\\\n U&=\\iint_{\\mathbb R^3\\times\\mathbb R^3}\n       \\frac{\\phi(y)^2\\phi(y')^2}{|y-y'|}\\,\\,\\mathrm dy\\,\\,\\mathrm dy',\n       \\qquad \\phi(y)=\\pi^{-1/2}e^{-|y|}.\n \\end{split}$$",
            "source_id": "PM-275.1"
          },

            "anchor": "eq:onsite-integral",
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-source** [claim](#stmt-thm-source) / [proof](#stmt-thm-source): Import the QMA-complete square-lattice Heisenberg problem and normalize rational data -> source Hamiltonian with polynomial scales and inverse-polynomial gap
- **s-second-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-second-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-order): Two-sided variational comparison with the second-order effective operator -> bottom-energy error bound
- **s-positive-spin** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-spin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-spin): Apply the perturbation lemma to singlet-mediator ancilla pairs -> positive-coupling spin Hamiltonian with scalar shift
- **s-contact-layout** [claim](#stmt-lem-contact-layout) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-contact-layout): Case analysis of distances between gadget sites -> complete list of unit contacts
- **s-displacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-displacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-displacement): Accumulate along rows, columns and runs to build a linear right inverse -> bounded displacements realizing prescribed length changes
- **s-hubbard** [claim](#stmt-prop-hubbard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hubbard): Superexchange by second-order perturbation plus hopping rounding -> half-filled Hubbard model with rational hoppings
- **s-isolated-well** [claim](#stmt-prop-isolated-well) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-isolated-well): Hardy inequality, Kato-Rellich, compactness and perturbation of the hydrogenic operator -> uniform well estimates and simple ground state
- **s-secants** [claim](#stmt-lem-wells-secants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wells-secants): First-order expansion and variational principle with Hardy control of moving pole -> monotone bracketing and secant bounds
- **s-well-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-well-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-well-tail): Exponential barrier comparison and cutoff energy estimate -> crude exponential tails
- **s-analytic** [claim](#stmt-lem-wells-analytic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wells-analytic): Local elliptic estimates and a derivative recurrence -> geometric Taylor error bounds
- **s-ritz-approximation** [claim](#stmt-lem-wells-ritz-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wells-ritz-approximation): Graded dyadic grid, partition of unity, local Taylor polynomials and cutoffs -> exponentially accurate Ritz space
- **s-ritz-oracle** [claim](#stmt-prop-ritz-oracle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ritz-oracle): Exact rational matrices, certified Coulomb entries and exact inertia bisection -> certified eigenvalue enclosure
- **s-tuning** [claim](#stmt-prop-tuning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tuning): Contraction of coordinate-wise certified bisection -> rational secondary offsets
- **s-relative-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-relative-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-relative-tail): Sub- and supersolution barriers with capped remote fields -> relative orbital tail formula
- **s-surrogate** [claim](#stmt-lem-surrogate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-surrogate): Rescaling of capped kernels and integration along rays -> surrogate field replacement
- **s-positive-hopping** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-hopping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-hopping): Insert relative tails and compare secondary with primary kernel -> hole integral equals explicit hopping integral
- **s-reference-hopping** [claim](#stmt-lem-reference-hopping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-hopping): Triangle inequality for the upper bound, fixed-ball integration for the lower bound -> size of reference integrals
- **s-hopping-displacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-hopping-displacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hopping-displacement): Compare integrands pointwise, Taylor expansion of the distance, transverse tail estimate -> exponential displacement law
- **s-hopping-quadrature** [claim](#stmt-lem-hopping-quadrature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hopping-quadrature): Mesh with exact positive weights and Lipschitz control of the logarithm -> relative-accuracy integral approximation
- **s-hopping-orientation** [claim](#stmt-lem-hopping-orientation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hopping-orientation): Self-adjoint pairing of eigen-equations and tail overlap estimate -> orientation identity and smallness
- **s-calibration** [claim](#stmt-prop-calibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-calibration): Logarithmic increments fed to the displacement right inverse, then tuning -> calibrated rational centers
- **s-one-body-matrix** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-body-matrix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-body-matrix): Residual and Gram estimates, orthonormalization by functional calculus -> orthonormal one-particle matrix near the hopping matrix
- **s-complement-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-complement-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-complement-gap): Spatial localization identity with leakage along local ground states -> gap on the one-particle complement
- **s-fermion-hardy** [claim](#stmt-lem-fermion-hardy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fermion-hardy): Density-matrix kinetic trace and pairwise Hardy inequality -> dimension-independent bounds
- **s-continuum-elimination** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuum-elimination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuum-elimination): Many-particle gap on the complement and quadratic square completion -> exponentially small leakage error
- **s-coulomb-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuum-coulomb-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuum-coulomb-compression): Lipschitz coefficients, truncation to disjoint supports, onsite integral -> onsite Hubbard repulsion with small error
- **s-continuum-simulation** [claim](#stmt-prop-continuum-simulation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-continuum-simulation): Combine one-body, interaction and elimination errors in physical units -> Hubbard minimum simulated by the physical infimum
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-34): Ordered classical construction, affine threshold map, promise preservation -> deterministic polynomial-time reduction

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-charge-scale**: The integer charge scale obtained as the floor of the truncated exponential series sits within the factor-two window around the exponential of the length scale, so it is a legal exponentially large integer charge scale computed with exact rational arithmetic and no comparison against a transcendental number.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-ground-state-solver**: Treating the charge-scale routine as evidence that the electronic ground energy of the output molecule can be computed classically. -> The routine only produces one integer parameter; the hardness statement concerns the spectral infimum of the output instance, which is never computed by the reduction.
- **anti-projected-lower-bound**: Reusing the finite exact-arithmetic checks to claim lower bounds for a projected finite-basis Hamiltonian. -> A projected matrix only gives a variational upper bound on the infimum, so a NO bound does not transfer.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The reduction contains one fully explicit finite construction, the choice of the integer charge scale by a truncated exponential series in exact rational arithmetic; this step is implemented and checked. The analytic continuum estimates are not finite and are not tested here.

```python
# INPUTS: one positive integer D read from standard input.
# PRECONDITIONS: D is a positive integer, large enough for the tail estimate to hold.
# OUTPUTS: one integer, the floor of the truncated exponential series, printed on standard output.
# TIME: polynomial in D, with a number of exact rational operations linear in D.
# SPACE: polynomial in D bits, since all partial sums are exact rationals.
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Python fractions; no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and gives a nonzero exit status.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def main():
    D = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
    s = Fraction(0)
    term = Fraction(1)
    for j in range(0, 4*D+1):
        s += term
        term = term * D / (j+1)
    print(s.numerator // s.denominator)

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a worst-case hardness statement, so it supplies no library or API specification. The reduction is a long multi-stage numerical pipeline that cannot be used as drop-in software. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Hamiltonian is a clamped-nucleus electronic energy with exponentially scaled nuclear charges, so it is not a physics engine or real-time simulation model. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The certified one-well eigenvalue enclosure is a reusable numerical component: exact rational matrices, exact inertia tests and bisection give rational enclosures of a ground eigenvalue without assuming a spectral gap of the Ritz matrix. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | stated | The main theorem is a deterministic classical polynomial-time many-one reduction. It consists of finite models, a charge scale, calibrated positions, well tuning by a contraction, and affine rational thresholds. It is adaptable rather than directly implementable because it relies on large asymptotic constants. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper specifies an input encoding rather than a data structure. Rational data are signed binary numerator and denominator pairs, charges are binary and the electron number is unary. No structure with operations is analysed. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No circuit, parallel layout or hardware cost is analysed. The only computational resource statements are polynomial bit and time bounds on a classical machine. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Hardness is worst-case over promised instances of a quantum complexity class. It gives no average-case or key-level security assumption and no cryptographic primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, dataset or training procedure appears. The setting has no supplied orbital basis and no external field, so there is no learned surrogate or model class. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The energy infimum is a decision-problem target. The only iterative scheme is a contraction used to tune secondary nuclear parameters, which is not an optimisation or scheduling model. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reduction is a single-machine deterministic map. There is no communication, consensus or fault model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The remaining relevant area is quantum chemistry, and the paper explicitly limits what it says there. The theorem does not cover bounded nuclear charges, and projecting onto supplied orbitals gives only an upper bound on the ground energy. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-binary-charges-essential** (hypothesis): The reduction needs unrestricted positive nuclear charges encoded in binary, with exponentially large charges and inverse separations. It gives no hardness under an additional bound on nuclear charges. Blocked use: ['Concluding hardness for ordinary molecules with bounded nuclear charges', 'Concluding hardness for unary-encoded charges from this theorem']
- **lim-no-qma-membership** (formal_scope): The theorem is a hardness statement only. It does not assert membership in QMA, so it does not give QMA-completeness of the problem. Blocked use: ['Calling the continuum problem QMA-complete on the strength of this theorem']
- **lim-threshold-not-spectral-gap** (formal_scope): The threshold separation is a precision requirement on the promise. It is not a molecular spectral gap, a discrete eigenvalue at the infimum, or an efficiently preparable ground state. Blocked use: ['Assuming a normalizable ground state exists at the infimum', 'Using the separation as a gap for phase-estimation style state preparation']
- **lim-promise-only** (edge_case): Correctness is guaranteed only on instances satisfying the YES or NO promise. Outside the promise nothing is required. Blocked use: ['Reading the reduction output as a decision for instances in the gap between a and b']
- **lim-projection-upper-bound-only** (approximation): Projection onto supplied orbitals gives only an upper bound on the ground energy. A NO lower bound must exclude all omitted continuum states, and a projected NO instance does not transfer. Blocked use: ['Inferring hardness or lower bounds for finite-basis projected Hamiltonians from this result']
- **lim-no-binding-promise** (formal_scope): The reduction imposes no strict-binding promise, so the excess-charge ionization bound supplies neither the continuum exclusion estimate nor a ground-state existence assertion. Blocked use: ['Using the result to claim a molecule is bound or that a bound ground state exists']
- **lim-scale-large-D** (hypothesis): The well and calibration estimates hold only for a sufficiently large integer scale D with m/D small. Small D or many sites relative to D are outside the regime. Blocked use: ['Applying the well estimates at small length scales', 'Running the tuning and oracle stages when m/D is not small']
- **lim-tuning-fixed-charges** (hypothesis): The tuning stage presupposes that the rounded integer charges and the rational centers are already fixed. Changing a charge or center after tuning invalidates the tuned energies. Blocked use: ['Re-rounding charges or moving centers after well tuning without retuning']
- **lim-oracle-single-well** (edge_case): The certified eigenvalue enclosure is an oracle for one-electron operators with rational data of bounded bit length. It does not diagonalize the spin or Hubbard Hamiltonians and queries no many-electron energy. Blocked use: ['Using the enclosure to compute many-electron ground energies']
- **lim-nonconstructive-ground-state** (nonconstructive): Existence of the single-well ground eigenfunction comes from a minimizing sequence and compactness. It is used only to prove approximation, never as algorithm input. Blocked use: ['Treating the eigenfunction as a computable input of the reduction']
- **lim-source-gap-needed** (hypothesis): The Heisenberg-to-positive-spin stage needs an inverse-polynomial lower bound on the source gap and a large enough ancilla scale, namely the condition on the square root of Delta against V. Blocked use: ['Applying the positive-spin gadget without the stated scale condition or with no gap lower bound']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 275 (`lean/docs/275.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Continuum Coulomb hardness with binary nuclear charges](../../preprints/Continuum-Coulomb-hardness-with-binary-nuclear-charges-September-24-2026/Continuum-Coulomb-hardness-with-binary-nuclear-charges-September-24-2026.pdf)
> - [QMA-hardness of continuum Coulomb energy with unit nuclear charges](../../preprints/QMA-hardness-of-continuum-Coulomb-energy-with-unit-nuclear-charges-September-24-2026/QMA-hardness-of-continuum-Coulomb-energy-with-unit-nuclear-charges-September-24-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves QMA-hardness of approximating the electronic Coulomb spectral infimum in three-dimensional continuum space when positive integer nuclear charges are encoded in binary. The instances have distinct rational nuclear positions and a unary electron count, and the energy ranges over antisymmetric continuum states and all spin sectors. A deterministic polynomial-time many-one reduction produces the promise gap with threshold separation at least one, while keeping the complete output length polynomial. The same Comparator file also contains the companion unit-charge result.
>
> The formalization proves QMA-hardness of approximating the electronic ground-energy infimum for clamped unit-charge nuclei in the full spinful fermionic continuum. The reduction is deterministic and polynomial time, with rational nuclear positions and separated rational energy thresholds. No orbital basis, magnetic field, additional external potential, or binding premise is part of the input.
>
> The same Comparator file also includes the companion hardness result when positive integer nuclear charges are encoded in binary.

Comparator source `ContinuumCoulombHardness.lean` (20703 bytes, sha256 `aef4f4ebda8fc13ab772618abcebd4e2c67d5f25af41ba87b804f80fb3a22666`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open MeasureTheory
open scoped BigOperators

namespace ContinuumCoulomb.BinaryEncoding

abbrev BitString := List Bool

structure Codec (α : Type*) where
  encode : α → BitString
  read : BitString → α × BitString
  read_encode : ∀ x tail, read (encode x ++ tail) = (x, tail)

def encodeDigits : BitString → BitString
  | [] => [false]
  | b :: bs => true :: b :: encodeDigits bs

def readDigits : BitString → BitString × BitString
  | true :: b :: bs => let (digits, tail) := readDigits bs; (b :: digits, tail)
  | false :: bs => ([], bs)
  | _ => ([], [])

theorem readDigits_encode (digits tail : BitString) :
    readDigits (encodeDigits digits ++ tail) = (digits, tail) := by
  induction digits with
  | nil => rfl
  | cons b digits ih => simp only [encodeDigits, List.cons_append, readDigits, ih]

def decodeDigits : BitString → ℕ
  | [] => 0
  | b :: bs => Nat.bit b (decodeDigits bs)

theorem decodeDigits_bits (n : ℕ) : decodeDigits n.bits = n := by
  induction n using Nat.binaryRec' with
  | zero => rfl
  | bit b n h ih => simp only [Nat.bits_append_bit n b h, decodeDigits, ih]

def natural : Codec ℕ where
  encode n := encodeDigits n.bits
  read s := let (digits, tail) := readDigits s; (decodeDigits digits, tail)
  read_encode n tail := by simp only [readDigits_encode, decodeDigits_bits]

def encodeUnary : ℕ → BitString
  | 0 => [false]
  | n + 1 => true :: encodeUnary n

def readUnary : BitString → ℕ × BitString
  | true :: s => let (n, tail) := readUnary s; (n + 1, tail)
  | false :: s => (0, s)
  | [] => (0, [])

theorem readUnary_encode (n : ℕ) (tail : BitString) :
    readUnary (encodeUnary n ++ tail) = (n, tail) := by
  induction n with
  | zero => rfl
  | succ n ih => simp only [encodeUnary, List.cons_append, readUnary, ih]

def unary : Codec ℕ where
  encode := encodeUnary
  read := readUnary
  read_encode := readUnary_encode

def integer : Codec ℤ where
  encode
    | Int.ofNat n => false :: natural.encode n
    | Int.negSucc n => true :: natural.encode n
  read
    | false :: s => let (n, tail) := natural.read s; (Int.ofNat n, tail)
    | true :: s => let (n, tail) := natural.read s; (Int.negSucc n, tail)
    | [] => (0, [])
  read_encode z tail := by
    cases z <;> simp only [List.cons_append, natural.read_encode]

def pair {α β : Type*} (a : Codec α) (b : Codec β) : Codec (α × β) where
  encode x := a.encode x.1 ++ b.encode x.2
  read s := let (x, t) := a.read s; let (y, u) := b.read t; ((x, y), u)
  read_encode x tail := by
    rcases x with ⟨x, y⟩
    simp only [List.append_assoc, a.read_encode, b.read_encode]

def readCount {α : Type*} (a : Codec α) : ℕ → BitString → List α × BitString
  | 0, s => ([], s)
  | n + 1, s => let (x, t) := a.read s; let (xs, u) := readCount a n t; (x :: xs, u)

theorem readCount_encode {α : Type*} (a : Codec α) (xs : List α) (tail : BitString) :
    readCount a xs.length (xs.flatMap a.encode ++ tail) = (xs, tail) := by
  induction xs with
  | nil => rfl
  | cons x xs ih =>
    simp only [List.length_cons, List.flatMap_cons, List.append_assoc, readCount, a.read_encode, ih]

def list {α : Type*} (a : Codec α) : Codec (List α) where
  encode xs := natural.encode xs.length ++ xs.flatMap a.encode
  read s := let (n, t) := natural.read s; readCount a n t
  read_encode xs tail := by
    simp only [List.append_assoc, natural.read_encode, readCount_encode]

def equiv {α β : Type*} (a : Codec α) (e : β ≃ α) : Codec β where
  encode x := a.encode (e x)
  read s := let (x, t) := a.read s; (e.symm x, t)
  read_encode x tail := by simp only [a.read_encode, e.symm_apply_apply]

def sum {α β : Type*} (a : Codec α) (b : Codec β) : Codec (α ⊕ β) where
  encode
    | Sum.inl x => false :: a.encode x
    | Sum.inr y => true :: b.encode y
  read
    | false :: s => let (x, t) := a.read s; (Sum.inl x, t)
    | true :: s => let (y, t) := b.read s; (Sum.inr y, t)
    | [] => let (x, t) := a.read []; (Sum.inl x, t)
  read_encode x tail := by
    cases x <;> simp only [List.cons_append, a.read_encode, b.read_encode]

end ContinuumCoulomb.BinaryEncoding

namespace Coulomb

open scoped ENNReal Classical NNReal ComplexConjugate

abbrev Space := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)
abbrev Configuration (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n × Fin 3)
abbrev Spins (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Fin 2

structure Nuclei (M : ℕ) where
  nonempty : 0 < M
  position : Fin M → Space
  distinct : Function.Injective position
  charge : Fin M → ℝ
  charge_ge_one : ∀ j, 1 ≤ charge j

noncomputable def position {n : ℕ} (x : Configuration n) (i : Fin n) : Space :=
  WithLp.toLp 2 (fun b => x (i, b))

noncomputable def coulombKernel (x : Space) : ℝ := ‖x‖⁻¹

noncomputable def attraction {M : ℕ} (S : Nuclei M) (x : Space) : ℝ :=
  ∑ j, S.charge j * coulombKernel (x - S.position j)

noncomputable def permute {n : ℕ} (p : Equiv.Perm (Fin n))
    (x : Configuration n) : Configuration n :=
  WithLp.toLp 2 (fun ib => x (p ib.1, ib.2))

structure H1Vector (n : ℕ) where
  value : Spins n → Configuration n → ℂ
  gradient : Spins n → (Fin n × Fin 3) → Configuration n → ℂ
  value_L2 : ∀ s, MemLp (value s) 2 volume
  partial_L2 : ∀ s a, MemLp (gradient s a) 2 volume
  weak_partial : ∀ s a (φ : Configuration n → ℝ),
    ContDiff ℝ (⊤ : ℕ∞) φ → HasCompactSupport φ →
      (∫ x, value s x * (fderiv ℝ φ x (EuclideanSpace.single a 1) : ℂ)) =
        -(∫ x, gradient s a x * (φ x : ℂ))

def Antisymmetric {n : ℕ} (ψ : H1Vector n) : Prop :=
  ∀ (p : Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (s : Spins n),
    ∀ᵐ x ∂volume,
      ψ.value (s ∘ p) (permute p x) = ((p.sign : ℤ) : ℂ) * ψ.value s x

noncomputable def mass {n : ℕ} (ψ : H1Vector n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ s, ∫ x, ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2

noncomputable def kinetic {n : ℕ} (ψ : H1Vector n) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∑ s, ∑ a, ∫ x, ‖ψ.gradient s a x‖ ^ 2

noncomputable def nuclearEnergy {M n : ℕ} (S : Nuclei M) (ψ : H1Vector n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ s, ∫ x, (∑ i, attraction S (position x i)) * ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2

noncomputable def pairEnergy {n : ℕ} (ψ : H1Vector n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ s, ∫ x, (∑ i : Fin n, ∑ j : Fin n,
    if i < j then coulombKernel (position x i - position x j) else 0) *
      ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2

noncomputable def form {M n : ℕ} (S : Nuclei M) (ψ : H1Vector n) : ℝ :=
  kinetic ψ - nuclearEnergy S ψ + pairEnergy ψ

end Coulomb

namespace ContinuumCoulomb

open BinaryEncoding Matrix
open scoped Kronecker

abbrev SourceSpinBasis (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Fin 2

def sourceTensor (n : ℕ) (M : Fin n → Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) :
    Matrix (SourceSpinBasis n) (SourceSpinBasis n) ℂ := fun s t => ∏ i, M i (s i) (t i)

section

theorem bellScale_atLeastTwo : Nat.AtLeastTwo (1 + 1) :=
  @Nat.instAtLeastTwoHAddOfNat 1 (@Nat.instNeZeroSucc 0)

attribute [local instance] bellScale_atLeastTwo

def bellScale : ℂ := (Real.sqrt (1 / 2 : ℝ) : ℂ)

end

inductive QMAGate where
  | hadamard (qubit : ℕ)
  | phaseT (qubit : ℕ)
  | controlledNot (control target : ℕ)
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

def qmaGateEquiv : QMAGate ≃ ((ℕ ⊕ ℕ) ⊕ (ℕ × ℕ)) where
  toFun
    | .hadamard i => Sum.inl (Sum.inl i)
    | .phaseT i => Sum.inl (Sum.inr i)
    | .controlledNot i j => Sum.inr (i, j)
  invFun
    | Sum.inl (Sum.inl i) => .hadamard i
    | Sum.inl (Sum.inr i) => .phaseT i
    | Sum.inr (i, j) => .controlledNot i j
  left_inv g := by cases g <;> rfl
  right_inv g := by rcases g with (i | i) | ⟨i, j⟩ <;> rfl

def qmaGateCodec : Codec QMAGate := BinaryEncoding.equiv
  (BinaryEncoding.sum (BinaryEncoding.sum natural natural) (pair natural natural)) qmaGateEquiv

structure QMACircuit where
  work : ℕ
  witness : ℕ
  gates : List QMAGate
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

def qmaCircuitEquiv : QMACircuit ≃ (ℕ × (ℕ × List QMAGate)) where
  toFun c := (c.work, c.witness, c.gates)
  invFun c := ⟨c.1, c.2.1, c.2.2⟩
  left_inv c := by cases c; rfl
  right_inv c := by rcases c with ⟨n, w, gs⟩; rfl

def qmaCircuitCodec : Codec QMACircuit := BinaryEncoding.equiv
  (pair natural (pair natural (BinaryEncoding.list qmaGateCodec))) qmaCircuitEquiv

def QMAGate.WellFormed (n : ℕ) : QMAGate → Prop
  | .hadamard i => i < n
  | .phaseT i => i < n
  | .controlledNot i j => i < n ∧ j < n ∧ i ≠ j

def QMACircuit.WellFormed (c : QMACircuit) : Prop :=
  c.witness ≤ c.work ∧ ∀ g ∈ c.gates, g.WellFormed (c.work + 1)

def qmaHadamard : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ :=
  bellScale • !![1, 1; 1, -1]

def qmaPhaseT : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ :=
  !![1, 0; 0, bellScale * (1 + Complex.I)]

def qmaQubit (work index : ℕ) : Fin (work + 1) :=
  ⟨index % (work + 1), Nat.mod_lt _ (Nat.succ_pos _)⟩

def qmaControlledNot (work : ℕ) (control target : Fin (work + 1))
    (s : SourceSpinBasis (work + 1)) : SourceSpinBasis (work + 1) :=
  fun i => if i = target ∧ s control = 1 then Equiv.swap (0 : Fin 2) 1 (s i) else s i

def qmaGateMatrix (work : ℕ) : QMAGate →
    Matrix (SourceSpinBasis (work + 1)) (SourceSpinBasis (work + 1)) ℂ
  | .hadamard i => sourceTensor (work + 1)
      (fun k => if k = qmaQubit work i then qmaHadamard else 1)
  | .phaseT i => sourceTensor (work + 1)
      (fun k => if k = qmaQubit work i then qmaPhaseT else 1)
  | .controlledNot i j => fun s t =>
      if s = qmaControlledNot work (qmaQubit work i) (qmaQubit work j) t then 1 else 0

def qmaCircuitMatrix (c : QMACircuit) :
    Matrix (SourceSpinBasis (c.work + 1)) (SourceSpinBasis (c.work + 1)) ℂ :=
  c.gates.foldl (fun M g => qmaGateMatrix c.work g * M) 1

def qmaInitialState (c : QMACircuit) (hc : c.WellFormed)
    (psi : EuclideanSpace ℂ (SourceSpinBasis c.witness)) :
    SourceSpinBasis (c.work + 1) → ℂ :=
  fun s => if ∀ i : Fin (c.work + 1), c.witness ≤ i.val → s i = 0 then
    psi (fun i => s ⟨i.val, lt_of_lt_of_le i.isLt (hc.1.trans (Nat.le_succ _))⟩)
  else 0

def qmaAcceptance (c : QMACircuit) (hc : c.WellFormed)
    (psi : EuclideanSpace ℂ (SourceSpinBasis c.witness)) : ℝ :=
  ∑ s : SourceSpinBasis (c.work + 1),
    if s (Fin.last c.work) = 1 then
      Complex.normSq ((qmaCircuitMatrix c).mulVec (qmaInitialState c hc psi) s) else 0

structure QuantumVerifier where
  generate : BitString → QMACircuit
  uniform : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime id qmaCircuitCodec.encode generate
  size : Polynomial ℕ
  size_bound : ∀ x, (generate x).work + (generate x).gates.length ≤ size.eval x.length
  wellFormed : ∀ x, (generate x).WellFormed

structure PromiseProblem (α : Type) where
  yes : Set α
  no : Set α
  disjoint : @Disjoint (Set α)
    (@ChainCompletePartialOrder.toPartialOrder (Set α)
      (@ChainCompletePartialOrder.instOfCompleteLattice (Set α)
        (@CompleteBooleanAlgebra.toCompleteLattice (Set α)
          (@CompleteAtomicBooleanAlgebra.toCompleteBooleanAlgebra (Set α)
            (@Set.instCompleteAtomicBooleanAlgebra α)))))
    (@HeytingAlgebra.toOrderBot (Set α)
      (@Order.Frame.toHeytingAlgebra (Set α)
        (@CompleteDistribLattice.toFrame (Set α)
          (@CompleteBooleanAlgebra.toCompleteDistribLattice (Set α)
            (@CompleteAtomicBooleanAlgebra.toCompleteBooleanAlgebra (Set α)
              (@Set.instCompleteAtomicBooleanAlgebra α))))))
    yes no

section

theorem inQMA_two_atLeastTwo : Nat.AtLeastTwo (1 + 1) :=
  @Nat.instAtLeastTwoHAddOfNat 1 (@Nat.instNeZeroSucc 0)

theorem inQMA_three_atLeastTwo : Nat.AtLeastTwo (2 + 1) :=
  @Nat.instAtLeastTwoHAddOfNat 2 (@Nat.instNeZeroSucc 1)

attribute [local instance] inQMA_two_atLeastTwo inQMA_three_atLeastTwo

def InQMA (P : PromiseProblem BitString) : Prop :=
  ∃ V : QuantumVerifier,
    (∀ x ∈ P.yes, ∃ psi : EuclideanSpace ℂ (SourceSpinBasis (V.generate x).witness),
      ‖psi‖ = 1 ∧ 2 / 3 ≤ qmaAcceptance (V.generate x) (V.wellFormed x) psi) ∧
    (∀ x ∈ P.no, ∀ psi : EuclideanSpace ℂ (SourceSpinBasis (V.generate x).witness),
      ‖psi‖ = 1 → qmaAcceptance (V.generate x) (V.wellFormed x) psi ≤ 1 / 3)

end

structure PolynomialManyOne {α β : Type} (ea : α → BitString) (eb : β → BitString)
    (P : PromiseProblem α) (Q : PromiseProblem β) where
  map : α → β
  polynomialTime : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime ea eb map
  maps_yes : ∀ x ∈ P.yes, map x ∈ Q.yes
  maps_no : ∀ x ∈ P.no, map x ∈ Q.no

def QMAHard {α : Type} (encoding : α → BitString) (Q : PromiseProblem α) : Prop :=
  ∀ P : PromiseProblem BitString, InQMA P → Nonempty (PolynomialManyOne id encoding P Q)

structure BinaryRational where
  numerator : ℤ
  denominator : ℕ
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

def BinaryRational.value (q : BinaryRational) : ℚ :=
  (q.numerator : ℚ) / q.denominator

def binaryRationalEquiv : BinaryRational ≃ (ℤ × ℕ) where
  toFun q := (q.numerator, q.denominator)
  invFun q := ⟨q.1, q.2⟩
  left_inv q := by cases q; rfl
  right_inv q := by cases q; rfl

def binaryRationalCodec : Codec BinaryRational := BinaryEncoding.equiv (pair integer natural) binaryRationalEquiv

abbrev Position := Coulomb.Space
abbrev Configuration := Coulomb.Configuration
abbrev SpinConfiguration := Coulomb.Spins

structure NuclearData where
  nuclei : ℕ
  nuclei_pos : 0 < nuclei
  position : Fin nuclei → Fin 3 → ℚ
  distinct : Function.Injective position
  charge : Fin nuclei → ℕ
  charge_pos : ∀ a, 0 < charge a
  electrons : ℕ
  electrons_pos : 0 < electrons

structure UnitNuclearData where
  nuclei : ℕ
  nuclei_pos : 0 < nuclei
  position : Fin nuclei → Fin 3 → ℚ
  distinct : Function.Injective position
  electrons : ℕ
  electrons_pos : 0 < electrons

def UnitNuclearData.toNuclearData (d : UnitNuclearData) : NuclearData where
  nuclei := d.nuclei
  nuclei_pos := d.nuclei_pos
  position := d.position
  distinct := d.distinct
  charge := fun _ => 1
  charge_pos := fun _ => Nat.zero_lt_one
  electrons := d.electrons
  electrons_pos := d.electrons_pos

section

theorem realPosition_atLeastTwo : Nat.AtLeastTwo (1 + 1) :=
  @Nat.instAtLeastTwoHAddOfNat 1 (@Nat.instNeZeroSucc 0)

attribute [local instance] realPosition_atLeastTwo

def realPosition (r : Fin 3 → ℚ) : Position :=
  WithLp.toLp 2 (fun a => (r a : ℝ))

end

theorem realPosition_injective : Function.Injective realPosition := by
  intro r s hrs
  funext a
  have h := congrArg (fun p : Position => p a) hrs
  change (r a : ℝ) = (s a : ℝ) at h
  exact_mod_cast h

def NuclearData.toNuclei (d : NuclearData) : Coulomb.Nuclei d.nuclei where
  nonempty := d.nuclei_pos
  position := fun a => realPosition (d.position a)
  distinct := realPosition_injective.comp d.distinct
  charge := fun a => (d.charge a : ℝ)
  charge_ge_one := by
    intro a
    exact_mod_cast d.charge_pos a

abbrev FormState (n : ℕ) := {state : Coulomb.H1Vector n // Coulomb.Antisymmetric state}

abbrev normSq {n : ℕ} (state : FormState n) := Coulomb.mass state.val
abbrev kineticEnergy {n : ℕ} (state : FormState n) := Coulomb.kinetic state.val

def coulombForm (d : NuclearData) (state : FormState d.electrons) : ℝ :=
  Coulomb.form d.toNuclei state.val

def unitCoulombForm (d : UnitNuclearData) (state : FormState d.electrons) : ℝ :=
  coulombForm d.toNuclearData state

def groundEnergy (d : NuclearData) : EReal :=
  sInf {e | ∃ state : FormState d.electrons,
    normSq state = 1 ∧ e = (coulombForm d state : EReal)}

def unitGroundEnergy (d : UnitNuclearData) : EReal :=
  sInf {e | ∃ state : FormState d.electrons,
    normSq state = 1 ∧ e = (unitCoulombForm d state : EReal)}

structure BinaryPosition where
  x : BinaryRational
  y : BinaryRational
  z : BinaryRational
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

def BinaryPosition.value (p : BinaryPosition) : Fin 3 → ℚ := ![p.x.value, p.y.value, p.z.value]

def BinaryPosition.Valid (p : BinaryPosition) : Prop :=
  0 < p.x.denominator ∧ 0 < p.y.denominator ∧ 0 < p.z.denominator

def binaryPositionEquiv : BinaryPosition ≃ (BinaryRational × (BinaryRational × BinaryRational)) where
  toFun p := (p.x, p.y, p.z)
  invFun p := ⟨p.1, p.2.1, p.2.2⟩
  left_inv p := by cases p; rfl
  right_inv p := by rcases p with ⟨x, y, z⟩; rfl

def binaryPositionCodec : Codec BinaryPosition := BinaryEncoding.equiv
  (pair binaryRationalCodec (pair binaryRationalCodec binaryRationalCodec)) binaryPositionEquiv

structure BinaryCoulomb where
  nuclei : List (BinaryPosition × ℕ)
  electrons : ℕ
  lower : BinaryRational
  upper : BinaryRational
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

def binaryCoulombEquiv : BinaryCoulomb ≃
    (List (BinaryPosition × ℕ) × (ℕ × (BinaryRational × BinaryRational))) where
  toFun d := (d.nuclei, d.electrons, d.lower, d.upper)
  invFun d := ⟨d.1, d.2.1, d.2.2.1, d.2.2.2⟩
  left_inv d := by cases d; rfl
  right_inv d := by rcases d with ⟨ns, n, a, b⟩; rfl

def binaryCoulombCodec : Codec BinaryCoulomb := BinaryEncoding.equiv
  (pair (BinaryEncoding.list (pair binaryPositionCodec natural))
    (pair unary (pair binaryRationalCodec binaryRationalCodec))) binaryCoulombEquiv

structure BinaryCoulomb.Valid (d : BinaryCoulomb) : Prop where
  nuclei_pos : 0 < d.nuclei.length
  electrons_pos : 0 < d.electrons
  positions : ∀ a : Fin d.nuclei.length, (d.nuclei.get a).1.Valid
  distinct : Function.Injective (fun a : Fin d.nuclei.length => (d.nuclei.get a).1.value)
  charge_pos : ∀ a : Fin d.nuclei.length, 0 < (d.nuclei.get a).2
  lower_denominator : 0 < d.lower.denominator
  upper_denominator : 0 < d.upper.denominator
  gap : 1 ≤ d.upper.value - d.lower.value

def BinaryCoulomb.toData (d : BinaryCoulomb) (h : d.Valid) : NuclearData where
  nuclei := d.nuclei.length
  nuclei_pos := h.nuclei_pos
  position a := (d.nuclei.get a).1.value
  distinct := h.distinct
  charge a := (d.nuclei.get a).2
  charge_pos := h.charge_pos
  electrons := d.electrons
  electrons_pos := h.electrons_pos

structure UnitCoulomb where
  nuclei : List BinaryPosition
  electrons : ℕ
  lower : BinaryRational
  upper : BinaryRational
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

def unitCoulombEquiv : UnitCoulomb ≃ (List BinaryPosition × (ℕ × (BinaryRational × BinaryRational))) where
  toFun d := (d.nuclei, d.electrons, d.lower, d.upper)
  invFun d := ⟨d.1, d.2.1, d.2.2.1, d.2.2.2⟩
  left_inv d := by cases d; rfl
  right_inv d := by rcases d with ⟨ns, n, a, b⟩; rfl

def unitCoulombCodec : Codec UnitCoulomb := BinaryEncoding.equiv
  (pair (BinaryEncoding.list binaryPositionCodec)
    (pair unary (pair binaryRationalCodec binaryRationalCodec))) unitCoulombEquiv

structure UnitCoulomb.Valid (d : UnitCoulomb) : Prop where
  nuclei_pos : 0 < d.nuclei.length
  electrons_pos : 0 < d.electrons
  positions : ∀ a : Fin d.nuclei.length, (d.nuclei.get a).Valid
  distinct : Function.Injective (fun a : Fin d.nuclei.length => (d.nuclei.get a).value)
  lower_denominator : 0 < d.lower.denominator
  upper_denominator : 0 < d.upper.denominator
  gap : 1 ≤ d.upper.value - d.lower.value

def UnitCoulomb.toData (d : UnitCoulomb) (h : d.Valid) : UnitNuclearData where
  nuclei := d.nuclei.length
  nuclei_pos := h.nuclei_pos
  position a := (d.nuclei.get a).value
  distinct := h.distinct
  electrons := d.electrons
  electrons_pos := h.electrons_pos

def binaryCoulombPromise : PromiseProblem BinaryCoulomb where
  yes := {d | ∃ h : d.Valid, groundEnergy (d.toData h) ≤ ((d.lower.value : ℝ) : EReal)}
  no := {d | ∃ h : d.Valid, ((d.upper.value : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ groundEnergy (d.toData h)}
  disjoint := by
    apply Set.disjoint_left.mpr
    rintro d ⟨h, hyes⟩ ⟨h', hno⟩
    have hgap : d.lower.value < d.upper.value := by linarith [h.gap]
    have hgap' : ((d.lower.value : ℝ) : EReal) < ((d.upper.value : ℝ) : EReal) := by exact_mod_cast hgap
    exact (not_le_of_gt hgap') (hno.trans hyes)

def unitCoulombPromise : PromiseProblem UnitCoulomb where
  yes := {d | ∃ h : d.Valid, unitGroundEnergy (d.toData h) ≤ ((d.lower.value : ℝ) : EReal)}
  no := {d | ∃ h : d.Valid, ((d.upper.value : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ unitGroundEnergy (d.toData h)}
  disjoint := by
    apply Set.disjoint_left.mpr
    rintro d ⟨h, hyes⟩ ⟨h', hno⟩
    have hgap : d.lower.value < d.upper.value := by linarith [h.gap]
    have hgap' : ((d.lower.value : ℝ) : EReal) < ((d.upper.value : ℝ) : EReal) := by exact_mod_cast hgap
    exact (not_le_of_gt hgap') (hno.trans hyes)

theorem unit_coulomb_qmaHard : QMAHard unitCoulombCodec.encode unitCoulombPromise := by
  sorry

theorem binary_coulomb_qmaHard : QMAHard binaryCoulombCodec.encode binaryCoulombPromise := by
  sorry

end ContinuumCoulomb

end

end OAI
```
